# Source register - TR-2026.09

This register catalogs every file delivered for the release, maps each raw column to its canonical field and states the encoding convention of every column. The catalog is authoritative for which files belong to the release: a release whose catalog lists a file that is not present must not be published.

## Delimited file conventions

CSV files use ',' and TSV files a tab; quoting follows RFC 4180 (a quoted field may contain the delimiter, quotes doubled, or line breaks). Lines starting with '#' are comments, except '#CONTROL|' lines which carry partition controls (partition|row_count|hash_total). Blank lines carry nothing. A line whose cells are exactly column names of the file (in any order, using any documented alias) restates the header: the following lines use that order. Trailing empty cells beyond the header width are ignored. Files are UTF-8, optionally with a byte order mark; a line that is not valid UTF-8 is ISO-8859-1. Line ends may be LF or CRLF. JSON Lines files hold one object per line; objects with a '_meta' or '_control' key are not records ('_control' objects carry partition controls). Log files are line based and follow the same per-line encoding rule.

## Fixed-width conventions

Legacy core-banking batches are ISO-8859-1 fixed-width records. Column positions below are 0-based start and exclusive end. Record type 'D' is a detail record; 'H' is a header; 'T' is a trailer control record laid out as T + partition (13 characters, YYYY-MM-DDTHH) + row count (9 digits) + hash total (15 digits). Amounts are signed integers in minor units. Trailing spaces are padding.

## XML conventions

XML exports are UTF-8. Element text may use CDATA sections or character entities, attribute values may use character entities, and decimal attribute values may use a decimal comma. <Control> elements carry partition controls as attributes partition, rows and hash.

## INI, YAML, TOML and JSON

INI section names may be padded with spaces inside the brackets. YAML and TOML strings may be single- or double-quoted; JSON strings may use \u escapes. Any of these files, and the Markdown documents, may start with a UTF-8 byte order mark.

## Table footnotes

A document table may be followed by footnotes of the form 'Footnote: override <caption>; <key>; <column>; <value>' with the four parts separated by '; ' (for example 'Footnote: override Merchant offsets; MR-01234; offset; 0.125'). The key is the row's first column, or, in a table whose first column repeats, its first two columns joined by '/' (for example 'ORBIS/4837A1'). Such a footnote replaces that cell of the row whose first column equals the key; it is part of the table. Numeric cells of document tables may use a decimal comma.

## Mailbox conventions

The dispute notice mailbox is an mbox file. Message bodies may be quoted-printable or base64 encoded; the Date header carries the sending time with its UTC offset. Notices are ordered by that instant.

Footnote: convention iso_offset is an ISO-8601 instant with a UTC offset; epoch_s and epoch_ms count seconds and milliseconds since 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z; local_account and local_merchant are wall-clock times in the zone of the account's postal prefix or of the merchant; terminal_clock is UTC as kept by the terminal (add the terminal's skew_seconds); minor is an integer in minor units; major is a decimal in major units ('.' decimal, ',' grouping allowed); major_eu uses ',' for decimals and '.', a space or U+202F for grouping; minor_signed is a sign followed by 12 digits.
